In any case, it is unacceptable for taxpayers to have to go to their pockets once again to pay for tax rebates on taxes that are already too low in the North Sea. This is why the Enhedslisten cannot support this bill.

There have been things that should be weighed against each other. A very large billion is going to be missing, perhaps up to 26 billion. kr. etc. So we entered into the negotiations and said: then we will have an impact on how this is to be financed. I don't have any more time, but it's just to say that I actually have a pretty nuanced view of this. I do not think this is a very bad agreement; I do not think it is a really fantastic agreement. But from all the elements we entered it with our eyes open, and we are behind it. That is why, of course, we also support the draft law.

ParlaMint-DK_20171012184842	Thank you. I would like to start by thanking you for the overall positive reception of the proposal and for the discussion that has taken place here today in the Parliamentary Chamber. This is not surprising because a broad agreement has been reached on political support for the North Sea Agreement. And why is there a broad majority for that? Well, it is there because it is important for companies making billions of investments in future oil and gas recovery in the North Sea that they know that there is broad and stable political support for the tax conditions of their investment. Otherwise, we will not get them to invest. The first part of the agreement is the implementation of a temporary tax relief for companies in the North Sea. These are some concessions to ensure that a two-digit billion amount is invested in oil and gas extraction in the North Sea, among others. by ensuring full and complete reconstruction of the Tyrah field. The government has a general objective that Denmark should be independent of fossil fuels, i.e. coal, oil and gas typical. We have also put on years: 2050. It is, of course, a bit long-term, but it is also an ambitious objective. And until we are in the goal, it is in turn the government's wish that Denmark should be self-sufficient with fossil fuels as far as possible. There are a number of good reasons for this, and I would like to highlight them here when dealing with the legislative proposal. First of all, security of supply benefits that we in Denmark can continue to be independent of energy from unstable regions of the world, especially if we move a little more eastwards. Oil and gas production in the North Sea also creates many Danish jobs. And then that's right, as Mr. Beckett did. Rune Lund says that one can, of course, discuss how many jobs are involved, but that certainly creates some. And last but not least, oil and gas production in Denmark generates a large billion revenue for society and the Treasury. As Minister for Taxation, I attach great importance to the fact that the tax rules are designed in a way that best supports these three general objectives. When last year in the summer of 2016, it became clear that the Danish Underground Consortium, due to low oil prices, did not find it profitable to rebuild the facility on the Tyra field, nor was it a situation that the government could close its eyes to. That is why the government entered into dialogue with those affected by the new rules we are discussing today. The North Sea Agreement, and thus also the bill, temporarily relaxes taxation in what can be called an investment window covering the period 2017-25. Capital investment during this period will benefit from increased and accelerated depreciation, increased and accelerated deductions and increased and accelerated hydrocarbon deductions. It is temporary tax concessions to ensure that it is attractive for private companies operating in the North Sea and producing oil and gas extraction that it will be done in full in the coming years. The alternative is that the state is missing out on potential billions of revenue, as a number of rapporteurs have mentioned, and that industry is at risk of having to cut local jobs. The socio-economic balance is then ensured by the introduction of a special additional tax, which means that the advantage which companies derive from tax relief is fully or partially repaid if the oil price is higher than expected and expected. The second main element of the draft law is the abolition of the so-called VAT scheme, which is, after all, a financing we are relaxing for the North Sea. We will then finance it with the abolition of the VAT scheme or a large part of the funding coming from there. It was introduced in the 1980s to promote exports, and the scheme means that companies with negative VAT liability for a longer period are mainly exporting companies which can obtain an interest-free loan from the State. The economic situation today is fortunately, one can say significantly different than when the scheme was introduced. And I have also been informed by SKAT that other companies that are more at risk of bankruptcy than actual export companies are showing increasing interest in using the scheme. Therefore, there is indeed a risk of losses in the scheme. The scheme is also in danger of being exposed to fraud, among other things. Because, at the start-up stage, companies can obtain an amount on the basis of a budget and an expected negative VAT liability, and when the National Audit Office has also criticised SKAT's control of the VAT area, at least here, when we reduce and close the system, the possibility that some could use it unintentionally is removed. Overall, the assessment is that the risk of loss of the scheme is increasing, while the scheme is neither economic nor socio-economic. That is why we are proposing the abolition of the system per year. This Regulation shall enter into force on the twentieth day following that of its publication in the Official To ensure that all companies in the scheme are aware of the proposal, SKAT has recently sent out information to all the companies concerned. Finally, I can say that I am grateful for the debate that has taken place and, of course, I will answer any questions that may be raised here. This is, of course, a technical area, and I therefore fully understand that a large number of technical questions are being asked in the course of the committee's deliberations, and we will try to answer them both within the deadlines, but also fully, towards the second and third readings of the legislative proposal.

ParlaMint-DK_20171012190220	Thank you, President. I am also the deputy rapporteur on this bill for our tax rapporteur, Mr. Jesper Petersen. Nevertheless, we take pleasure in the fact that the agreement actually reached was significantly better than the crooked proposal originally presented by the government. The specific legislative proposal contains a number of minor initiatives which form part of the agreement. It provides that the requirement for actual sleeping places for motorhomes to be reduced from four to two, a change in the taxable value of veteran vehicles, a rule that the use of test signs and test marks should be changed and that the rules on foreign exchange rental vehicles should be moved from regulation to law level. A public servant who is required to check or remove a vehicle as part of his profession shall be able to drive an unregistered vehicle. In future, the defence must be able to be authorised to register and unsubscribe its own vehicles from the Vehicle Register. And then there is the adjustment of tax rates on taxable gas. The Social Democratic Party has no comment on the specific elements of this bill and is sensitive to the purpose of the bill, which is: the draft law is generally intended to ensure that the rules in the motor sector are up-to-date. The draft law therefore contains a number of minor initiatives, all of which are in common, namely the removal of inappropriateities and barriers or simplification of rules. There is nothing of it in which we can see any problems, and that is why the Social Democratic Party supports the bill.

ParlaMint-DK_20171012191540	Before going into my speech, I should like to say that I have been asked to greet the Radicals who support this proposal. But that doesn't bind the SF, I'd like to say. But now to my speech on behalf of the SF: we can easily see some advantages in this proposal, because that is not the way it should be. It is an advantage that gas as a propellant becomes cheaper than today. That is quite clear. Then it will be set up to correspond to the diesel. It's a truth with modifications, and I'll just come back to that. However, we would like the gas tax to be designed socially and socio-economically optimally so that we also support the transition to heavy-duty gas vehicles when we look forward to a future in which gas can be produced as renewable energy, unlike natural gas, and we will address that in committee. The latest Eurostat survey shows that the Danish transport sector is below the EU average measured on the RE share, and this actually gives a very good picture of how far we are still lagging behind in this area and how much challenges there are in transport. This summer, the Climate Council published a report on how Denmark is the easiest, cheapest and fastest to achieve the 2030 climate targets, i.e. the climate targets that we have in the area. It is not someone we have been charged with, but some we, as a Parliament, have actively said we would have. One of the key recommendations is to start the green transition in heavy transport, that is, where it does not yet make sense to be on electricity, but where it makes the best sense to stay on gas. Here the Climate Council concludes that it is socio-economically cheaper to switch diesel-powered trucks to gas-powered trucks than not to switch at all, and that point has been underlined by Ea Energy Analyses, which has estimated that the socio-economic gain by replacing a diesel-powered truck with a biogas truck will be approx. 40,000 kroner. annual. Before it becomes too boring with too many figures, I would just like to emphasise that the socio-economic aspect is not about the need to equate or even. However, it is, of course, a question of converting gas into heavy vehicles as soon as possible. The aim of this bill is, after all, to align the rates of diesel fuel and pure biogas for transport purposes. That is the purpose of this draft law and it aims to create a level playing field and to contribute to the conversion of heavy transport. Unfortunately, it happened this summer when we voted for L 210 and we are all accomplices to that, because we have all voted in favour of changing the fuel consumption base for gas-powered vehicles, which meant that the starting point for CO2 emissions was to be taken as a starting point, since gas vehicles need 10-15 per cent. More energy to drive 1 km than a diesel-powered vehicle, this change meant that gas vehicles were practically placed at a disadvantage than in the past, and even with this bill today it does not compensate for the distortion. The Climate Council has also highlighted in their recent report that there is a socio-economic gain of approx. 70 øre per. mileage for a truck that is switched from the diesel to the gas drive. The calculations thus show that the tax minister must listen because it is important for him that there is a positive government revenue effect of 37 cents per year. mileage. It is also a kind of money for such a shift. In short, we would like to challenge from the SF that there is real equality with this bill. We fully support the fact that this is a clear improvement, but we would like to go a little further in committee on the calculation methods. For it turns out that there is still a distortion, and especially when we look not only at the green gains, but the socio-economic gains, it is much cheaper that we get the heavy transport to gas more quickly. So it is both a good economic business, and we are also a little busy if we are to achieve the 2030 targets. So we will examine this a little further in committee. Either way, this is progress. We just do not think that this is as much progress as the draft law describes.

ParlaMint-DK_20171012192108	The aim of the bill is to update a number of rules in the motor sector to ensure that the rules become more appropriate and are experienced more reasonably and flexibly by the citizen. A good example of such an appropriate update is, of course, the fact that the number of sleeping places in a motorhome is adjusted from four to two, thus giving citizens better opportunities to buy a motorhome if they, for example, do so. only need two sleeping places, against today, when the requirement is four. This will be seen by the citizen as a good and debureaucratic update of the legislation. In addition, the draft law proposes that the taxable value of a veterinary vehicle be set at the commercial price, subject to a maximum limit. 40% of the registration fee. This makes it so easier to calculate the tax on the vehicle. At the same time, it is also ensured that it does not exceed the maximum. We must be priced at a commercial level and therefore not be higher than conventional cars, which are not vintage cars, which is also the intention. So it is also a very good proposal. In addition, there are a number of minor initiatives, including: on test marks and tax exemptions for certain vehicles, etc. I will not go into that in detail, but just say that we naturally support the Conservative People's Party's bill.
ParlaMint-DK_20171012192233	There are no brief comments, so thank you to the rapporteur. And then we give the floor to the tax minister.
ParlaMint-DK_20171012192238	Thank you. First of all, I would like to thank you for agreeing to the bill from a large number of parliamentary parties. As several rapporteurs have said, it is, of course, a series of necessary updates in the field of motor vehicles to ensure that the rules are appropriate and fair, hopefully for the benefit of citizens, businesses and the public sector as such. The draft law contains a number of perhaps minor initiatives, but it does have in common that they can have a major impact on the individual. And I think it is important and, to that extent, also necessary that we not only devote our energies to the major fiscal issues, but also have our rules reviewed from time to time to see whether they continue to work properly or whether they can be adjusted for the benefit of all. That is why I am very pleased that the legislative proposal reduces the requirement for sleeping places in motorhomes from four to two, so that we have more standardised rules. Today it is the case that most motorhomes sold in Denmark are equipped with four sleeping places instead of the two with which they are supplied from the factory, and we should be wary of making such special demands. So such a change is for the benefit of the citizen who only needs two sleeping places but who would otherwise have had to buy a motorhome with four sleeping places for tax purposes. I am also pleased that with the bill we are adapting the rules on test signs and test marks and that we are adjusting the taxable value of a veteran car so that as a citizen we do not risk paying registration fees for a higher tax base than the general rules on used cars. In particular, the test signs and test marks have been the subject of a lot of debate, which is why we are responding to those who have wanted a more lenient practice. I also think it has to be said that there has been a slightly uneven practice in relation to a part of this area, which is, moreover, an area which has not always been the subject of the SKAT area, but has previously been the subject of the police. There you have seen a number of areas and I think you can also see the outlines of here that it can vary slightly from department to department, from police circle to police circle, how you understand the rules. But SKAT has then taken over the task, and now we also have a solid legislative basis to stand on and a uniform practice, but let us not forget that there is room for those who need test signs and test marks to have access to them on a reasonable basis. In short, I am satisfied with the legislative proposal that we have tabled otherwise we would not have tabled it either. And I'll be happy to answer any questions that may be asked. I should just like to make a brief comment on Mr Seligman's report. Rune Lund, who may give the impression that this was a major violation of the Constitution or a major breach of the Constitution. Sir. Rune Lund has been in the bill on page 7 and read about it, and that is fine, but the fact that something is now being moved from regulation to legislation is not necessarily the same as the fact that the rules are contrary to the constitution. This is only an expression of the need to exercise the utmost vigilance when it comes to the constitution. So it's to wear a belt and braces, if you like. The draft law on page 7 of Section 3.3.2 also states that this constitutional position concerns the use of test marks and test marks, and what has been proposed here does not, I believe, concern the fact that some people have paid too much, but more the practice of handing out test marks and test signs to some people who then do not pay registration fees in whole or in part. When you give people such an opportunity to drive around a car under specially limited conditions without paying a tax, this is a discrimination against others, and you can argue for such discrimination if you have to wear a life strap and seat belts. And that's what we're gonna do this way. I don't think so, but we would like to reply to that in committee that this is a major breach of the constitution.
